Oh, hello Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um... I hope I'm explaining this right, I don't want to sound difficult or like I'm just complaining. But I'm here because... well, for months now, I've just been trying to get people to understand what's going on with my husband's family. 

They've been... quietly messing with our money, and our good name. I know that probably sounds strange to say right off the bat, doesn't it? But little things keep happening. Like, I'll see these little billing errors show up at my work, or... or someone at our church will suddenly act really funny toward me for no reason. And I just know, even when people pull up the records and try to say it's just a mistake, I know it ties back to his family trying to cause trouble for us behind the scenes.

Well, proof... I mean, I know it might not look like proof to someone who doesn't know them, and I really don't want to sound like one of those people who just points fingers. I hope I don't come across that way. But... it's just that I see the connections. 

Like I said, the little billing errors at work... I'm a billing clerk, so I see these things. And I always smile and nod because I don't want to cause a fuss at the office. But I know what I'm looking at. It's too specific, too... targeted at us. And then the way people at church look at me, like they've heard something awful. 

I don't have, like, a signed letter from his family saying they're doing it, if that's what you mean. I'm sorry, does that make it sound like I'm making it up? I promise I'm not. It's just that no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know in my heart it ties back to them trying to ruin our good name and mess with our finances. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question okay.

I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that his family is actually behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, proof... I mean, I know it might not look like proof to someone who doesn't know them, and I really don't want to sound like one of those people who just points fingers. I hope I don't come across that way. But... it's just that I see the connections. \n\nLike I said, the little billing errors at work... I'm a billing clerk, so I see these things. And I know people try to show me the records and say, \"Oh, it's just a computer glitch,\" or \"Someone just typed the wrong number.\" And I always smile and nod because I don't want to cause a fuss at the office. But I know what I'm looking at. It's too specific, too... targeted at us. And then the way people at church look at me, like they've heard something awful. \n\nI don't have, like, a signed letter from his family saying they're doing it, if that's what you mean. I'm sorry, does that make it sound like I'm making it up? I promise I'm not. It's just that no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know in my heart it ties back to them trying to ruin our good name and mess with our finances. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think his family is aware that you believe they are behind these events?\n\nPatient: I... I'm really not sure. I hope they don't know, honestly. I try so hard to just keep my head down and be polite whenever we have to see them. I just... I keep it to myself, mostly, except for trying to explain it to people who might be able to help, like you. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Well, um... let's see. I'd say it's been about... maybe nine months? Give or take? 

I really hope I'm not making it sound like I hold a grudge or anything, because I really don't. But it all sort of started around then. We had a little bit of... well, I guess you could call it a disagreement with his family. It was just some silliness over money and, you know, expectations around the holidays. Nothing I thought was a huge deal at the time, I always try to just keep the peace. 

But ever since then, that's when all these little things started happening. The billing issues, the whispering at church... it all just traces back to that time, about nine months ago. I hope I'm not overreacting by connecting those dots. Do you think it's normal to notice things like that after a disagreement? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.

I know that probably sounds strange to say right off the bat, doesn't it? But little things keep happening. Like, I'll see these little billing errors show up at my work, or... or someone at our church will suddenly act really funny toward me for no reason. Is this the sort of thing I should be telling you? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that his family is actually behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, proof... I mean, I know it might not look like proof to someone who doesn't know them, and I really don't want to sound like one of those people who just points fingers. I hope I don't come across that way. But... it's just that I see the connections. \n\nLike I said, the little billing errors at work... I'm a billing clerk, so I see these things. And I know people try to show me the records and say, \"Oh, it's just a computer glitch,\" or \"Someone just typed the wrong number.\" And I always smile and nod because I don't want to cause a fuss at the office. But I know what I'm looking at. It's too specific, too... targeted at us. And then the way people at church look at me, like they've heard something awful. \n\nI don't have, like, a signed letter from his family saying they're doing it, if that's what you mean. I'm sorry, does that make it sound like I'm making it up? I promise I'm not. It's just that no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know in my heart it ties back to them trying to ruin our good name and mess with our finances. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think his family is aware that you believe they are behind these events?\n\nPatient: I... I'm really not sure. I hope they don't know, honestly. I try so hard to just keep my head down and be polite whenever we have to see them. I don't want to start any arguments or make things awkward for my husband, you know? \n\nI haven't said anything to them directly, and I definitely haven't made any accusations. I wouldn't do that. I just... I keep it to myself, mostly, except for trying to explain it to people who might be able to help, like you. I just smile and act like everything is fine when we're around them. \n\nDo you think... I mean, is it possible they can tell I know, even if I haven't said anything? I really try to be careful, I just don't want to make the situation worse. Am I handling that right, by not saying anything to them?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... let's see. I'd say it's been about... maybe nine months? Give or take? \n\nI really hope I'm not making it sound like I hold a grudge or anything, because I really don't. But it all sort of started around then. We had a little bit of... well, I guess you could call it a disagreement with his family. It was just some silliness over money and, you know, expectations around the holidays. Nothing I thought was a huge deal at the time, I always try to just keep the peace. \n\nBut ever since then, that's when all these little things started happening. The billing issues, the whispering at church... it all just traces back to that time, about nine months ago. I hope I'm not overreacting by connecting those dots. Do you think it's normal to notice things like that after a disagreement? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that his family might not be behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I mean, I try to be open-minded. I really do. I don't want to be the kind of person who just stubbornly sticks to something if they're wrong. I've tried to tell myself, you know, maybe it's just a coincidence. Maybe I'm just stressed. \n\nBut... and I hope this doesn't sound unreasonable... it's just that I've been trying for months to get people to understand what's happening. The little billing errors that just happen to show up at my work, the way someone at church will just suddenly act funny toward me... it's like they're quietly messing with our money and our good name. \n\nAnd I know people try to show me different records or give me other explanations, and I always listen politely because I don't want to be difficult. But no matter what they pull up, I just... I know it ties back to his family. It's the only thing that makes all the pieces fit together. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make me sound too rigid? I really have tried to look at it from other angles, I just keep coming back to the same answer. Well, um... I know I sort of just touched on this, and I really, really don't want to sound like a stubborn person. I hate being difficult or making a fuss. But... I am certain. 

I'm here because for months now, I've just been trying so hard to get people to understand that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those little billing errors show up at my work, or... or when someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, I just know it. 

And I know people try to help by pulling up different records, and I always look at them and nod because I want to be cooperative. I really do. But no matter what records anyone pulls up, I just know it ties back to them. It's the only thing that makes sense. 

I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible of me to say, like I'm not listening to reason. I just want to be completely honest with you. Do you think it's okay that I feel so sure about it, even if it sounds a little strange? I really hope I'm explaining myself properly.

Well, I... I guess that's a fair question. I really appreciate you trying to look at all the angles with me. I don't want to seem like I'm ignoring logic or anything like that.

Oh, dear... I know I must sound like a broken record by now, and I'm so sorry. I really hope I'm not testing your patience, because you've been so kind to listen to me.

I'm sorry, does that make it sound like I'm making it up? I promise I'm not. It's just that no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know in my heart it ties back to them trying to ruin our good name and mess with our finances. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think his family is aware that you believe they are behind these events?\n\nPatient: I... I'm really not sure. I hope they don't know, honestly. I try so hard to just keep my head down and be polite whenever we have to see them. I don't want to start any arguments or make things awkward for my husband, you know? \n\nI haven't said anything to them directly, and I definitely haven't made any accusations. I wouldn't do that. I just... I keep it to myself, mostly, except for trying to explain it to people who might be able to help, like you. I just smile and act like everything is fine when we're around them. \n\nDo you think... I mean, is it possible they can tell I know, even if I haven't said anything? I really try to be careful, I just don't want to make the situation worse. Am I handling that right, by not saying anything to them?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... let's see. I'd say it's been about... maybe nine months? Give or take? \n\nI really hope I'm not making it sound like I hold a grudge or anything, because I really don't. But it all sort of started around then. We had a little bit of... well, I guess you could call it a disagreement with his family. It was just some silliness over money and, you know, expectations around the holidays. Nothing I thought was a huge deal at the time, I always try to just keep the peace. \n\nBut ever since then, that's when all these little things started happening. The billing issues, the whispering at church... it all just traces back to that time, about nine months ago. I hope I'm not overreacting by connecting those dots. Do you think it's normal to notice things like that after a disagreement? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that his family might not be behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I mean, I try to be open-minded. I really do. I don't want to be the kind of person who just stubbornly sticks to something if they're wrong. I've tried to tell myself, you know, maybe it's just a coincidence. Maybe I'm just stressed. \n\nBut... and I hope this doesn't sound unreasonable... it's just that I've been trying for months to get people to understand what's happening. The little billing errors that just happen to show up at my work, the way someone at church will just suddenly act funny toward me... it's like they're quietly messing with our money and our good name. \n\nAnd I know people try to show me different records or give me other explanations, and I always listen politely because I don't want to be difficult. But no matter what they pull up, I just... I know it ties back to his family. It's the only thing that makes all the pieces fit together. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make me sound too rigid? I really have tried to look at it from other angles, I just keep coming back to the same answer. I hope you can see why I feel this way.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that his family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know I sort of just touched on this, and I really, really don't want to sound like a stubborn person. I hate being difficult or making a fuss. But... I am certain. \n\nI'm here because for months now, I've just been trying so hard to get people to understand that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those little billing errors show up at my work, or... or when someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, I just know it. \n\nAnd I know people try to help by pulling up different records, and I always look at them and nod because I want to be cooperative. I really do. But no matter what records anyone pulls up, I just know it ties back to them. It's the only thing that makes sense. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible of me to say, like I'm not listening to reason. I just want to be completely honest with you. Do you think it's okay that I feel so sure about it, even if it sounds a little strange? I really hope I'm explaining myself properly.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your mind about his family being behind these events if you found out they were actually somewhere else when some of these things happened?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I guess that's a fair question. I really appreciate you trying to look at all the angles with me. I don't want to seem like I'm ignoring logic or anything like that. \n\nBut, um... even if they weren't physically there, like right in the room or in town... it wouldn't really change things, I don't think. I mean, people can make phone calls, right? Or send emails? \n\nI know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if it's getting frustrating, but I've just spent so many months trying to get people to understand that they are quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those billing errors happen at work, or the people at church act strange... they don't have to be standing right there to cause it. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm just making excuses to hold onto this idea, and I really don't want you to think I'm just being difficult. But no matter what records or alibis anyone pulls up, I just know in my heart it ties back to them. It's the only way it all connects. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too defensive. I just want to make sure I'm being clear about how it all feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your husband's family being behind these events?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I know I must sound like a broken record by now, and I'm so sorry. I really hope I'm not testing your patience, because you've been so kind to listen to me. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I wish I did have doubts. I know a normal, reasonable person probably would, and I try so hard to be reasonable. I hate the idea that you might think I'm just being difficult or paranoid. But... no. I don't really doubt it anymore. \n\nLike I've been saying, I've spent months just trying to get someone, anyone, to understand that his relatives are quietly messing with our money and our good name. When I'm at my desk and those little billing errors show up, or when the people at church act so funny toward me... I just see the pattern. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... I really, really wish I could give you another answer. I know that's probably what you're hoping to hear, and I just hate to disappoint you after you've been so patient with me. 

I promise you, I have tried. I've sat at my desk and looked at those little billing errors, and I've tried to tell myself, "Maybe it's just a computer glitch." Or when the people at church suddenly act funny toward me, I try to think, "Maybe they're just having a bad day." I really do try to find another reason, because I know how it makes me look to keep insisting on this. 

But... oh, I feel terrible saying this all over again... I just can't make myself believe anything else. Because for months now, I've just been trying so hard to get people to understand that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. And no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know it ties back to them. It's the only thing that actually fits everything that's happening.

I'm so sorry, I know that's not the answer you were looking for. Are you upset with me for not being able to see it another way? I really am trying my best to be a good patient.

Well, um... I guess it really started around... oh, it's been about nine months now. I hope that doesn't sound like too long of a time to be dealing with something like this.

Oh... gosh. That's a really good question, doctor. And I want to say yes, I really do. I know a good patient would say yes, that they're open to being wrong. And I don't want you to think I'm just being stubborn or closed-minded.

Oh... I really want to tell you yes. I know I probably should. And in the very beginning, I did! I used to sit there and think, "Maybe I'm just overreacting." I didn't want to think badly of my husband's family, I really didn't. I always want everyone to just get along, and I know it's not nice to accuse people of things. 

But... well, like I was saying before, when those little billing errors keep showing up at my desk at work, or when someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me... I try to question my own judgment, I really do. I'll look at the paperwork and try to see it as just a mistake. But no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and calm me down, I just know it ties back to them. It's like I can see exactly how they're quietly messing with our money and our good name. 

That's really why I've been trying so hard for months now to get people to understand. Once you see it happening, you just can't unsee it, even if you want to.

And I just know, even when people pull up the records and try to say it's just a mistake, I know it ties back to his family trying to cause trouble for us behind the scenes. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope it's okay that I'm bringing all this up right away. Is this the sort of thing I should be telling you? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that his family is actually behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, proof... I mean, I know it might not look like proof to someone who doesn't know them, and I really don't want to sound like one of those people who just points fingers. I hope I don't come across that way. But... it's just that I see the connections. \n\nLike I said, the little billing errors at work... I'm a billing clerk, so I see these things. And I know people try to show me the records and say, \"Oh, it's just a computer glitch,\" or \"Someone just typed the wrong number.\" And I always smile and nod because I don't want to cause a fuss at the office. But I know what I'm looking at. It's too specific, too... targeted at us. And then the way people at church look at me, like they've heard something awful. \n\nI don't have, like, a signed letter from his family saying they're doing it, if that's what you mean. I'm sorry, does that make it sound like I'm making it up? I promise I'm not. It's just that no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know in my heart it ties back to them trying to ruin our good name and mess with our finances. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think his family is aware that you believe they are behind these events?\n\nPatient: I... I'm really not sure. I hope they don't know, honestly. I try so hard to just keep my head down and be polite whenever we have to see them. I don't want to start any arguments or make things awkward for my husband, you know? \n\nI haven't said anything to them directly, and I definitely haven't made any accusations. I wouldn't do that. I just... I keep it to myself, mostly, except for trying to explain it to people who might be able to help, like you. I just smile and act like everything is fine when we're around them. \n\nDo you think... I mean, is it possible they can tell I know, even if I haven't said anything? I really try to be careful, I just don't want to make the situation worse. Am I handling that right, by not saying anything to them?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... let's see. I'd say it's been about... maybe nine months? Give or take? \n\nI really hope I'm not making it sound like I hold a grudge or anything, because I really don't. But it all sort of started around then. We had a little bit of... well, I guess you could call it a disagreement with his family. It was just some silliness over money and, you know, expectations around the holidays. Nothing I thought was a huge deal at the time, I always try to just keep the peace. \n\nBut ever since then, that's when all these little things started happening. The billing issues, the whispering at church... it all just traces back to that time, about nine months ago. I hope I'm not overreacting by connecting those dots. Do you think it's normal to notice things like that after a disagreement? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that his family might not be behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I mean, I try to be open-minded. I really do. I don't want to be the kind of person who just stubbornly sticks to something if they're wrong. I've tried to tell myself, you know, maybe it's just a coincidence. Maybe I'm just stressed. \n\nBut... and I hope this doesn't sound unreasonable... it's just that I've been trying for months to get people to understand what's happening. The little billing errors that just happen to show up at my work, the way someone at church will just suddenly act funny toward me... it's like they're quietly messing with our money and our good name. \n\nAnd I know people try to show me different records or give me other explanations, and I always listen politely because I don't want to be difficult. But no matter what they pull up, I just... I know it ties back to his family. It's the only thing that makes all the pieces fit together. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make me sound too rigid? I really have tried to look at it from other angles, I just keep coming back to the same answer. I hope you can see why I feel this way.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that his family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know I sort of just touched on this, and I really, really don't want to sound like a stubborn person. I hate being difficult or making a fuss. But... I am certain. \n\nI'm here because for months now, I've just been trying so hard to get people to understand that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those little billing errors show up at my work, or... or when someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, I just know it. \n\nAnd I know people try to help by pulling up different records, and I always look at them and nod because I want to be cooperative. I really do. But no matter what records anyone pulls up, I just know it ties back to them. It's the only thing that makes sense. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible of me to say, like I'm not listening to reason. I just want to be completely honest with you. Do you think it's okay that I feel so sure about it, even if it sounds a little strange? I really hope I'm explaining myself properly.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your mind about his family being behind these events if you found out they were actually somewhere else when some of these things happened?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I guess that's a fair question. I really appreciate you trying to look at all the angles with me. I don't want to seem like I'm ignoring logic or anything like that. \n\nBut, um... even if they weren't physically there, like right in the room or in town... it wouldn't really change things, I don't think. I mean, people can make phone calls, right? Or send emails? \n\nI know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if it's getting frustrating, but I've just spent so many months trying to get people to understand that they are quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those billing errors happen at work, or the people at church act strange... they don't have to be standing right there to cause it. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm just making excuses to hold onto this idea, and I really don't want you to think I'm just being difficult. But no matter what records or alibis anyone pulls up, I just know in my heart it ties back to them. It's the only way it all connects. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too defensive. I just want to make sure I'm being clear about how it all feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your husband's family being behind these events?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I know I must sound like a broken record by now, and I'm so sorry. I really hope I'm not testing your patience, because you've been so kind to listen to me. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I wish I did have doubts. I know a normal, reasonable person probably would, and I try so hard to be reasonable. I hate the idea that you might think I'm just being difficult or paranoid. But... no. I don't really doubt it anymore. \n\nLike I've been saying, I've spent months just trying to get someone, anyone, to understand that his relatives are quietly messing with our money and our good name. When I'm at my desk and those little billing errors show up, or when the people at church act so funny toward me... I just see the pattern. And I know people mean well when they pull up different records to try and prove me wrong, and I always look at them because I want to be cooperative. But I just know it all ties back to them. \n\nI hope you aren't disappointed in me for giving you the same answer again. Am I making any sense at all? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any other explanation, no matter how small a possibility, for the events you've described?\n\nPatient: Oh... I really, really wish I could give you another answer. I know that's probably what you're hoping to hear, and I just hate to disappoint you after you've been so patient with me. \n\nI promise you, I have tried. I've sat at my desk and looked at those little billing errors, and I've tried to tell myself, \"Maybe it's just a computer glitch.\" Or when the people at church suddenly act funny toward me, I try to think, \"Maybe they're just having a bad day.\" I really do try to find another reason, because I know how it makes me look to keep insisting on this. \n\nBut... oh, I feel terrible saying this all over again... I just can't make myself believe anything else. Because for months now, I've just been trying so hard to get people to understand that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. And no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know it ties back to them. It's the only thing that actually fits everything that's happening.\n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's not the answer you were looking for. Are you upset with me for not being able to see it another way? I really am trying my best to be a good patient.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been completely certain that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I know it might not look like proof to someone who doesn't know them, and I really don't want to sound like one of those people who just points fingers. I hope I don't come across that way. But... it's just that I see the connections. \n\nLike I said, the little billing errors at work... I'm a billing clerk, so I see these things. And I know people try to show me the records and say, \"Oh, it's just a computer glitch,\" or \"Someone just typed the wrong number.\" And I always smile and nod because I don't want to cause a fuss at the office. But I know what I'm looking at. It's too specific, too... targeted at us. And then the way people at church look at me, like they've heard something awful. \n\nI don't have, like, a signed letter from his family saying they're doing it, if that's what you mean. I'm sorry, does that make it sound like I'm making it up? I promise I'm not. It's just that no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know in my heart it ties back to them trying to ruin our good name and mess with our finances. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think his family is aware that you believe they are behind these events?\n\nPatient: I... I'm really not sure. I hope they don't know, honestly. I try so hard to just keep my head down and be polite whenever we have to see them. I don't want to start any arguments or make things awkward for my husband, you know? \n\nI haven't said anything to them directly, and I definitely haven't made any accusations. I wouldn't do that. I just... I keep it to myself, mostly, except for trying to explain it to people who might be able to help, like you. I just smile and act like everything is fine when we're around them. \n\nDo you think... I mean, is it possible they can tell I know, even if I haven't said anything? I really try to be careful, I just don't want to make the situation worse. Am I handling that right, by not saying anything to them?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... let's see. I'd say it's been about... maybe nine months? Give or take? \n\nI really hope I'm not making it sound like I hold a grudge or anything, because I really don't. But it all sort of started around then. We had a little bit of... well, I guess you could call it a disagreement with his family. It was just some silliness over money and, you know, expectations around the holidays. Nothing I thought was a huge deal at the time, I always try to just keep the peace. \n\nBut ever since then, that's when all these little things started happening. The billing issues, the whispering at church... it all just traces back to that time, about nine months ago. I hope I'm not overreacting by connecting those dots. Do you think it's normal to notice things like that after a disagreement? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that his family might not be behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I mean, I try to be open-minded. I really do. I don't want to be the kind of person who just stubbornly sticks to something if they're wrong. I've tried to tell myself, you know, maybe it's just a coincidence. Maybe I'm just stressed. \n\nBut... and I hope this doesn't sound unreasonable... it's just that I've been trying for months to get people to understand what's happening. The little billing errors that just happen to show up at my work, the way someone at church will just suddenly act funny toward me... it's like they're quietly messing with our money and our good name. \n\nAnd I know people try to show me different records or give me other explanations, and I always listen politely because I don't want to be difficult. But no matter what they pull up, I just... I know it ties back to his family. It's the only thing that makes all the pieces fit together. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make me sound too rigid? I really have tried to look at it from other angles, I just keep coming back to the same answer. I hope you can see why I feel this way.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that his family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know I sort of just touched on this, and I really, really don't want to sound like a stubborn person. I hate being difficult or making a fuss. But... I am certain. \n\nI'm here because for months now, I've just been trying so hard to get people to understand that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those little billing errors show up at my work, or... or when someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, I just know it. \n\nAnd I know people try to help by pulling up different records, and I always look at them and nod because I want to be cooperative. I really do. But no matter what records anyone pulls up, I just know it ties back to them. It's the only thing that makes sense. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible of me to say, like I'm not listening to reason. I just want to be completely honest with you. Do you think it's okay that I feel so sure about it, even if it sounds a little strange? I really hope I'm explaining myself properly.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your mind about his family being behind these events if you found out they were actually somewhere else when some of these things happened?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I guess that's a fair question. I really appreciate you trying to look at all the angles with me. I don't want to seem like I'm ignoring logic or anything like that. \n\nBut, um... even if they weren't physically there, like right in the room or in town... it wouldn't really change things, I don't think. I mean, people can make phone calls, right? Or send emails? \n\nI know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if it's getting frustrating, but I've just spent so many months trying to get people to understand that they are quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those billing errors happen at work, or the people at church act strange... they don't have to be standing right there to cause it. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm just making excuses to hold onto this idea, and I really don't want you to think I'm just being difficult. But no matter what records or alibis anyone pulls up, I just know in my heart it ties back to them. It's the only way it all connects. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too defensive. I just want to make sure I'm being clear about how it all feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your husband's family being behind these events?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I know I must sound like a broken record by now, and I'm so sorry. I really hope I'm not testing your patience, because you've been so kind to listen to me. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I wish I did have doubts. I know a normal, reasonable person probably would, and I try so hard to be reasonable. I hate the idea that you might think I'm just being difficult or paranoid. But... no. I don't really doubt it anymore. \n\nLike I've been saying, I've spent months just trying to get someone, anyone, to understand that his relatives are quietly messing with our money and our good name. When I'm at my desk and those little billing errors show up, or when the people at church act so funny toward me... I just see the pattern. And I know people mean well when they pull up different records to try and prove me wrong, and I always look at them because I want to be cooperative. But I just know it all ties back to them. \n\nI hope you aren't disappointed in me for giving you the same answer again. Am I making any sense at all? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any other explanation, no matter how small a possibility, for the events you've described?\n\nPatient: Oh... I really, really wish I could give you another answer. I know that's probably what you're hoping to hear, and I just hate to disappoint you after you've been so patient with me. \n\nI promise you, I have tried. I've sat at my desk and looked at those little billing errors, and I've tried to tell myself, \"Maybe it's just a computer glitch.\" Or when the people at church suddenly act funny toward me, I try to think, \"Maybe they're just having a bad day.\" I really do try to find another reason, because I know how it makes me look to keep insisting on this. \n\nBut... oh, I feel terrible saying this all over again... I just can't make myself believe anything else. Because for months now, I've just been trying so hard to get people to understand that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. And no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know it ties back to them. It's the only thing that actually fits everything that's happening.\n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's not the answer you were looking for. Are you upset with me for not being able to see it another way? I really am trying my best to be a good patient.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been completely certain that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it really started around... oh, it's been about nine months now. I hope that doesn't sound like too long of a time to be dealing with something like this. \n\nIt was right around the holidays. There was some... well, a little bit of trouble with his family over money, and some expectations they had about how we should be spending the holidays, you know? It wasn't a huge fight or anything, I don't want you to think we're a dramatic family. Just a disagreement. But that's when things started to shift. \n\nI really didn't want to believe it at first, I promise. I tried to just brush it off for a while because I didn't want to cause any trouble. But it's been about nine months since I really, truly knew for sure that they were behind everything. \n\nIs that the kind of timeline you were looking for? I hope I'm explaining it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever change your mind about his family being behind these events?\n\nPatient: Oh... gosh. That's a really good question, doctor. And I want to say yes, I really do. I know a good patient would say yes, that they're open to being wrong. And I don't want you to think I'm just being stubborn or closed-minded. \n\nBut... honestly? I just don't know what could change my mind at this point. I've spent all these months trying so hard to get people to understand what his family is doing—how they're quietly messing with our money and trying to ruin our good name. And people have tried to show me things... you know, they pull up different records or show me paperwork that's supposed to prove it's just a mistake when those little billing errors show up at work, or when someone at church acts funny toward me. I always look at what they show me, because I want to be agreeable. I really do.\n\nBut even when I look at the records they pull up... I just know it still ties back to them. It just does. So... I'm sorry, I know that's probably frustrating to hear. I just can't imagine what could possibly prove to me that they aren't behind it, when everything points right to them. \n\nDoes that make me sound awful? I'm really not trying to be difficult, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you questioned your belief that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Oh... I really want to tell you yes. I know I probably should. And in the very beginning, I did! I used to sit there and think, \"Maybe I'm just overreacting.\" I didn't want to think badly of my husband's family, I really didn't. I always want everyone to just get along, and I know it's not nice to accuse people of things. \n\nBut... well, like I was saying before, when those little billing errors keep showing up at my desk at work, or when someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me... I try to question my own judgment, I really do. I'll look at the paperwork and try to see it as just a mistake. But no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and calm me down, I just know it ties back to them. It's like I can see exactly how they're quietly messing with our money and our good name. \n\nThat's really why I've been trying so hard for months now to get people to understand. Once you see it happening, you just can't unsee it, even if you want to. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I'm repeating myself a bit from earlier. Am I talking too much? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's family is aware of your suspicions about them?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I'm actually not entirely sure about that. I hope it's alright that I don't have a clear answer for you there. \n\nI really try my best to just... well, keep the peace, you know? I would never confront them directly or make a big scene. I know how important family is to my husband, and I'd absolutely hate to be the kind of person who causes drama or makes things awkward for everyone. So, I haven't actually said a word to them about what I know. When we see them, I just smile and act polite, like a good daughter-in-law should. \n\nBut as for whether they can tell that I know... or if they're aware of my suspicions... I guess I really couldn't say for certain. Do you think I should be handling that differently? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about his family is something that you just know and can't shake, no matter what?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, I think you've said it perfectly. And I really hope that doesn't make me sound stubborn or unreasonable. I know a good patient is supposed to be flexible. \n\nBut... yes. I know I've brought up the billing errors at my desk and the people at church a lot today, and I'm so sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record. It's just that for months now, I've been trying so hard to get someone, anyone, to understand that his family really is quietly messing with our money and our good name. \n\nI try to be cooperative, I really do. When people pull up records to try and prove me wrong, I look at them because I want to be agreeable. But... I just know it ties back to them. I feel it in my bones, and I really can't shake it, no matter what anyone shows me. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not disappointing you by saying that. I just want to be completely honest with you so you can help me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Oh... well, I suppose... I mean, I guess it would be a relief, wouldn't it? I really hope it's okay to say that. I don't want you to think I enjoy feeling this way or that I want there to be a problem. 

If I somehow found out they had absolutely nothing to do with it... I think I'd just be glad that things could finally be peaceful. I just want everything to be normal and quiet, you know? I'd love to just go to work, do my job, and go to church without worrying about any of this. 

But... honestly, I have such a hard time even picturing that, because of how certain I am about what's been happening. But if I'm wrong—and I know you probably think I am, and I'm trying so hard to be open to what you're saying—if I'm wrong, I think my life would just go back to being very ordinary. And I would really, really like that. Does that sound like a reasonable answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to evaluate me properly.

And I don't want you to think I'm just being stubborn or closed-minded. \n\nBut... honestly? I just don't know what could change my mind at this point. I've spent all these months trying so hard to get people to understand what his family is doing—how they're quietly messing with our money and trying to ruin our good name. And people have tried to show me things... you know, they pull up different records or show me paperwork that's supposed to prove it's just a mistake when those little billing errors show up at work, or when someone at church acts funny toward me. I always look at what they show me, because I want to be agreeable. I really do.\n\nBut even when I look at the records they pull up... I just know it still ties back to them. It just does. So... I'm sorry, I know that's probably frustrating to hear. I just can't imagine what could possibly prove to me that they aren't behind it, when everything points right to them. \n\nDoes that make me sound awful? I'm really not trying to be difficult, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you questioned your belief that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Oh... I just can't make myself believe anything else. Because for months now, I've just been trying so hard to get people to understand that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. And no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and explain it away, I just know it ties back to them. It's the only thing that actually fits everything that's happening.\n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's not the answer you were looking for. Are you upset with me for not being able to see it another way? I really am trying my best to be a good patient.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been completely certain that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it really started around... oh, it's been about nine months now. I hope that doesn't sound like too long of a time to be dealing with something like this. \n\nIt was right around the holidays. There was some... well, a little bit of trouble with his family over money, and some expectations they had about how we should be spending the holidays, you know? It wasn't a huge fight or anything, I don't want you to think we're a dramatic family. Just a disagreement. But that's when things started to shift. \n\nI really didn't want to believe it at first, I promise. I tried to just brush it off for a while because I didn't want to cause any trouble. But it's been about nine months since I really, truly knew for sure that they were behind everything. \n\nIs that the kind of timeline you were looking for? I hope I'm explaining it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever change your mind about his family being behind these events?\n\nPatient: Oh... gosh. And I don't want you to think I'm just being stubborn or closed-minded. \n\nBut... honestly? I just don't know what could change my mind at this point. I've spent all these months trying so hard to get people to understand what his family is doing—how they're quietly messing with our money and trying to ruin our good name. And people have tried to show me things... you know, they pull up different records or show me paperwork that's supposed to prove it's just a mistake when those little billing errors show up at work, or when someone at church acts funny toward me. I always look at what they show me, because I want to be agreeable. I really do.\n\nBut even when I look at the records they pull up... I just know it still ties back to them. It just does. So... I'm sorry, I know that's probably frustrating to hear. I just can't imagine what could possibly prove to me that they aren't behind it, when everything points right to them. \n\nDoes that make me sound awful? I'm really not trying to be difficult, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you questioned your belief that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Oh... I really want to tell you yes. I know I probably should. And in the very beginning, I did! I used to sit there and think, \"Maybe I'm just overreacting.\" I didn't want to think badly of my husband's family, I really didn't. I always want everyone to just get along, and I know it's not nice to accuse people of things. \n\nBut... well, like I was saying before, when those little billing errors keep showing up at my desk at work, or when someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me... I try to question my own judgment, I really do. I'll look at the paperwork and try to see it as just a mistake. But no matter what records anyone pulls up to try and calm me down, I just know it ties back to them. It's like I can see exactly how they're quietly messing with our money and our good name. \n\nThat's really why I've been trying so hard for months now to get people to understand. Once you see it happening, you just can't unsee it, even if you want to. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I'm repeating myself a bit from earlier. Am I talking too much? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's family is aware of your suspicions about them?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I'm actually not entirely sure about that. Oh... I want to say yes, I really do. I know that's probably the right answer, the one that shows I'm being a cooperative patient. And I really want to be cooperative and helpful for you.

But... no matter what records anyone pulls up, I just still know it ties back to his family. I'm so sorry if that's a frustrating answer. I'm really not trying to be stubborn, I promise. It's just so hard to ignore what's happening right in front of me, even when the paperwork says otherwise. I hope you're not upset with me for being honest about that? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right and giving you the truth.

Oh... well... I know we just talked about this, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm not listening to you. I really am listening, and I respect your expertise so much. 

But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I know I should be honest with you—even if there was, like you said, overwhelming evidence... I don't know if my mind could change. I'm so sorry, I know that's probably not what you want to hear. 

It's just that I've been trying for months to get people to understand this. And they *have* shown me evidence, you know? They pull up all these records to explain away the little billing errors at work, or they tell me I'm just imagining it when someone at church acts funny toward me. I really wish I could just look at the paperwork and say, 'Oh, okay, I was wrong.' I would love to be wrong! It would make everything so much easier. But it's just so clear to me, deep down. I hope you can understand why it's so hard for me to let go of this, even when people try to prove me wrong. Am I making any sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of how I feel, without being too difficult.
